#2225. Cray Wanderers vs Stockport County - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 06 Nov 2026 12:50, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 12:56

The oldest football club in London will hope to create history in the world's oldest cup competition on Sunday afternoon when seventh-tier Cray Wanderers welcome League One side Stockport County to a sold-out Flamingo Park.

The Wands have progressed through several qualifying rounds to reach the FA Cup First Round Proper, earning a tie against a Hatters side who climbed to second in the league after beating Wimbledon 2-1 last weekend.

Having played seven matches in the FA Cup this season, four of which ended level after 90 minutes, with three requiring replays, Cray Wanderers have reached the First Round Proper for the first time in their 166-year history, having been founded before the competition itself.

Entering the competition in the First Qualifying Round, the Wands overcame Lewes, Maidstone United, Chippenham Town and, most recently, Bedford Town to set up Sunday's historic encounter.

Led by Tim O'Shea, who made three league appearances for Tottenham Hotspur during the 1980s, Cray's most recent tie saw them draw with Bedford after 90 minutes before finding another equaliser in extra time to take the contest to a penalty shootout.

The Wands subsequently prevailed 4-3 on penalties to create a moment of history and have since returned to league action, where they compete at Step 3 of the National League System and currently sit just one place above the relegation zone in the Isthmian League Premier Division.

However, their league position does not fully reflect the four wins, three draws and three defeats they have recorded from their opening 10 matches, with their impressive FA Cup run forcing several fixtures to be postponed and rearranged.

As a result, O'Shea's side have as many as seven games in hand on some of their fellow seventh-tier sides.

As for Stockport County, they make the journey to Chislehurst looking to avoid what could prove to be one of the biggest FA Cup upsets of the season.

Led by Jimmy McNulty, the Hatters have enjoyed an impressive run of form in League One, with four victories from their last six matches lifting them to second in the table, just three points behind the league leaders.

Stockport's FA Cup campaign came to an end in the second round last season following a 5-4 penalty shootout defeat to Cambridge United, although they faced Premier League opposition in Crystal Palace during the 2024-25 campaign, eventually suffering a narrow 1-0 defeat to the eventual cup winners.

More recently, the Greater Manchester outfit have been adjusting to life under new manager McNulty after long-serving boss Dave Challinor departed in the summer following their playoff final defeat to Bolton Wanderers.

The Hatters are hoping to return to the second tier for the first time since 2002 and, with a long season still ahead, will be determined to keep their promotion ambitions on track while avoiding an unwanted cup exit this weekend.

Stockport County form (all competitions):

Ade Yussuff scored two of Cray's four goals in their most recent outing, and the striker, who has found the net 12 times this season, is expected to lead the line for the hosts on Sunday afternoon.

The Wands are also likely to retain JJ Briggs in goal, while captain Frankie Raymond is expected to continue at full-back.

As for visiting Stockport County, McNulty could make wholesale changes as his side prepare to face opponents who sit 111 league places below them in the football pyramid.

The Hatters boss may choose to rest key players such as Jack Diamond and Kyle Wootton, potentially opening the door for teenage forward Che Gardner and French striker Mikael Mandron to feature from the start.

Regular goalkeeper Oliver Whatmuff could also make way for Luca Ashby-Hammond, who has featured twice in the EFL Trophy this season.

Brad Hills and Kyron Gordon will, however, play no part in this weekend's cup tie due to injury.

Briggs; Lema, Strachan, Morgan, Raymond; McLeod, Adaja, Ogunwamide, Hernandez; Yusuff, Ufuah

Stockport County possible starting lineup:

Ashby-Hammond; Jobe, O'Connell, Pye; Dacres-Cogley, Norwood, Fiorini, Ennis; Gardner, Mandron, Edun

While Sunday promises to be a historic occasion for one of the country's oldest football clubs, we expect Stockport County to have far too much quality for their non-league opponents.

The League One outfit boast one of the most prolific attacks in the English Football League, and we expect McNulty's men to demonstrate the gulf in quality by securing a comfortable victory and booking their place in the next round.

#2224. Returning star makes 'up my game' admission amid striker battle

Joe Westley believes his battle for game time with fellow Dundee attackers will only benefit the Tayside club.

With just one defeat in the last five, the Dark Blues are in fifth place in the William Hill Premiership and the fourth-highest scorers, with Ashley Hay notching three in the last four games and Bradley Fink with four in the last six.

Westley missed three games with an ankle injury but replaced Fink in the 3-1 win over Kilmarnock last Wednesday and, ahead of the Tayside derby against St Johnstone at McDiarmid Park on Saturday, the 22-year-old, with five goals in all competitions this season, said: “I think it’s a healthy competition.

“Everyone wants to be playing football and be in the team. So when we’ve got Ash and Brad scoring, it means I need to up my game and score as many as I can when I’m given the chance because you know that when you’ve got the shirt, people are waiting to take it off you.

“It’s a healthy competition and I think we all drive each other to be the best versions of ourselves.

“I think when all the strikers are scoring and doing well, it’s good to be a part of an attack that’s doing well because you create for each other. If everyone’s firing and everyone’s doing well, then it increases your chance of getting goals and assisting as well.

“It’s a really good time to be an attacker at the club. I think it’s starting to click between everyone and whoever’s on up front has got a great understanding of what’s wanted from them. It gives them the best possible chance to score goals and win games.

“I think everything can come down to hard work. As a group everyone around the club works so hard day in, day out and we’re pushed in training, we’re pushed in the gym and I think we try and always gain the little one percent that we can every week.

“But also there’s the environment that the manager has created where everyone’s in it together. Everyone wants to do the hard yards for each other and I think the hard yards and the basics of football are things that can separate you from other teams in the league.

“I think so far this season we’ve been good at the basics and as long as we continue to do them we’ll be fine.”

#2220. CM: Dalot could reunite with Amorim at Milan as Man Utd deal winds down

Diogo Dalot has once again been linked with a potential return to AC Milan, where he spent the 2020-21 season on loan.

Calciomercato.com report that Roma’s visit to face Manchester United at Old Trafford will not only be an opportunity for the Giallorossi to face and perhaps erase the ghosts of the past, but also to watch someone up close who could be a name for the not-too-distant future: Diogo Dalot.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The Portuguese full-back’s contract is expiring at the end of next season and although he is happy in Manchester, many clubs are beginning to ask his entourage for details.

Roma were one step away from landing the defender in the summer of 2021 when the Portuguese coach José Mourinho was on the bench. The negotiations dragged on for a long time, but in the end it didn’t go through.

The reason ended up being quite simple: the Red Devils never agreed to a loan with option to buy formula while Roma – who were still severely limited by Financial Fair Play limitations – could not offer anything more.

Dalot knows Italy and Serie A well, and he recently spoke about his loan experience with Milan in an interview with The Player’s Tribune.

“It was one of the most important years of my career. I remember the way the club welcomed me and how welcome I felt in a year that was important to me, one in which I needed to regain my confidence in my football. Milan helped me get back to those levels,” he said.

“Every day, Paolo Maldini was on the sidelines to watch us train. Rain, shine, or cold, he was always there, in a suit and tie. He would say to me: ‘Mark like this’ or ‘Tackle it.’ Other times, he would scold me for trying too hard. ‘You’re an extraordinary player, don’t worry, you’ll see, everything will be fine’.”

As mentioned, Dalot’s deal expires in 2028 but there is the presence of a one-year clause that United can trigger. There are a number of teams monitoring his situation including Inter, Milan and Juventus, who have all enquired in recent weeks.

Nothing concrete has emerged yet, as Dalot happy in Manchester and isn’t thinking about the future. However, if things don’t change, a departure – perhaps even for a low-cost fee – could become a realistic possibility.

With the Rossoneri needing defenders, could they try pounce on a reunion for a versatile player that they and Ruben Amorim know well?

#2218. Celtic trigger Turkish TV meltdown as ‘rant of a lifetime’ takes bonkers turn

Celtic’s stunning Europa League comeback sent Turkish TV into meltdown as raging Besiktas pundits struggled to comprehend how their side had managed to lose in Glasgow.

Broadcaster Onur Yildiz absolutely savaged Martin O’Neill’s side and claimed they wouldn’t even finish in the TOP TEN in Turkey. And former Besiktas star Nihat Kahveci was hardly more complimentary, branding Celtic’s first-half display the worst he had ever seen from the Hoops and wondering how Besiktas possibly managed to turn such a comfortable night into defeat.

The Istanbul giants arrived in Glasgow unbeaten in the League Phase after a testing opening trio against Marseille, Hoffenheim and Crystal Palace. And for 45 minutes, they appeared to be cruising towards another positive result as Celtic struggled to lay a glove on them.

But the game was transformed after the break. The Hoops cranked up the tempo and deservedly came out on top to spark a furious post-mortem back in Turkey.

Read more: After missing out on another senior call-up, my Celtic form has been rewarded

And Yildiz led the inquest with an extraordinary rant which somehow stretched from Ottoman history to Vaclav Cerny’s infamous water bottle incident at Celtic Park.

Speaking on BTV, Yildiz fumed: “Samet, look. If Celtic played in our league, the Super Lig, and finished in the top ten, I’ll change my name.

“Look, Besiktas played against such a bad team today. During the first half, Nubel almost got pneumonia. The ball didn’t reach the man.

“So you take the lead in the second half. You’re saying it’s going to be like a tourist trip. A match you’ll win, and you’ll go home happy and cheerful. It’s like a friendly in an Austrian training camp.

“But something happens to Besiktas away from home, and suddenly the fans start tearing their hair out. Look. Corum, Kocaelispor, Kasimpasa. I dare say these are way, way better teams than Celtic.

“The guys came with a 0-3 record. They were coming to get their first point from us. They got their first win against us. So Besiktas played against such a bad team. It’s time we started talking about the shortcomings.

“So it’s over for Besiktas. The Tulip Period is over. First of all, Besiktas absolutely needed to sign a good central defender. The defence is falling apart. At the start of the season, Haric was the only kid who resisted. Apart from Emirhan, the defence is bad. Djalo is bad. Agbadou is bad. Besiktas conceded their first goal because of him.

“We were shouting from the rooftops that they needed a right-winger. There’s no right-winger either. Why did they keep Cerny on today? How did he last so long? He gets a bit more motivated in this game because of some past incidents.

“There’s an incident, I don’t know if you remember, Cerny was playing for Glasgow Rangers. Glasgow Rangers score a goal against Celtic, Cerny pulled a water bottle from the bench and sprayed it at the Celtic fans. That’s why he was booed every time the ball came to his feet.

“He was booed not only because he played for Rangers, but also because of that incident. But Besiktas’ flaws have now been revealed.”

#2216. Heerenveen vs Feyenoord - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adepoju Marvellous | 06 Nov 2026 12:55, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 20:51

Feyenoord will look to maintain their position at the top of the Eredivisie table when they face Heerenveen at the Abe Lenstra Stadion on Sunday.

Despite being separated by seven points and six places, the hosts enter this weekend’s match as arguably the more confident side, having won four straight matches, while the visitors are searching for their first victory in five outings.

Picking up where they left off before the September/October international break, Feyenoord resumed with wins over AZ Alkmaar, Como, and Telstar to extend their winning streak to five matches, and few would have predicted the difficult spell that has followed.

Champions League defeats to Real Betis and, most recently, Inter Milan - coupled with Eredivisie draws against PSV Eindhoven and Fortuna Sittard - have seen Giovanni van Bronckhorst’s men go four games without a win for the first time since January, when they went winless in six matches either side of the new year.

Despite their ongoing wobble, De Stadionclub still hold a slender lead at the top of the league and need to get back on track to maintain it. A defeat would not only end the division’s last remaining unbeaten record but could also drop them as low as fourth.

Since recording three straight wins between December 2021 and March 2024, Feyenoord have failed to beat Heerenveen in their last two away meetings and also suffered a KNVB Beker defeat to the same opponents last season.

With just two goals scored across their last four matches, Sunday’s visitors will first need to regain their attacking impetus, which has seen them average three goals per league game so far, second only to PSV’s 3.18.

While much of their opponents’ success this season has been down to attacking prowess, Heerenveen can lay claim to being one of the Eredivisie’s most defensively astute teams after 11 matches.

Second only to Twente and Feyenoord for fewest goals conceded, Robin Veldman’s men showcased their defensive solidity in a 1-0 victory over NEC last time out, securing a league-high fifth clean sheet.

De Superfriezen now boast wins in four straight competitive matches for the first time since September 2017, and a torrid run earlier this season, when they went five games without a victory, now seems a distant memory.

Only three teams in the division have collected more home points than Heerenveen’s 10, despite their modest return of eight goals in six matches. Facing a team without a clean sheet in six outings, they will be confident of adding to that tally.

Having broken the deadlock in five of their last six matches, Sunday’s visitors have made a habit of starting games as they mean to go, but so have Feyenoord, whose 33 goals have had nine of them come in the opening half hour.

Levi Smans marked his return to action for Heerenveen with an assist, and the hosts now boast a clean bill of health for the first time in some time.

Dylan Vente’s winner against NEC was his third goal in four matches, taking his tally to five direct goal contributions this season.

Jordan Bos, Jakub Moder, and Bart Nieuwkoop remain unavailable for Feyenoord due to knee injuries, while Gijs Smal is sidelined with an ankle problem.

Thomas Beelen and Jeremiah St. Juste complete the visitors’ list of absentees, with Beelen still awaiting his first appearance since July 2025.

Anis Hadj Moussa has been his side’s standout attacker this term, and despite an ineffective display against Inter in midweek, he remains one to watch.

Klaverboer; Braude, Kersten, Willemsen, Zagaritis; Linday, Courtens; Trenskow, Smans, Rivera; Vente

Ernst; Lopez, Marmol, Watanabe, Read; Targhalline, Vanhoutte, Zechiel; Valente, Ferri, Hadj Moussa

Feyenoord appear vulnerable at the moment, so there may be no better time for an in-form Heerenveen side to attempt an upset than this weekend.

That said, good teams are defined by their ability to respond during difficult spells, and we expect Feyenoord to return to winning ways on Sunday.

Camarda was present at the Paolo and Larissa Pini Institute for a one-on-one discussion with students and a lesson with the Sport and Health tutors, to help promote the value of sport and the desire to continue practicing it.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

It was the player who asked the students questions for the most part, not the other way around. Camarda questioned them on their motivations behind playing sports, if they have ever considered quitting and if so why they chose to continue.

After the discussion, the practical part of the program began, with a physical activity lesson led by Sport e Salute tutors, in collaboration with Adidas too. Camarda spoke to some reporters present at the event, with his words via MilanNews.

A post shared by MilanNews.it (@milannewsitofficial)

“That’s the beauty of sport: it gives you the chance to test yourself, have fun, and improve, even when you don’t succeed at something.

Camarda himself is having to be patient and to wait for opportunities at the moment given his lack of playing time under Ruben Amorim, which may even mean evaluating another loan move away from his boyhood club.

#2214. Spain told to walk away from 2030 World Cup if Morocco gets final

Spain should withdraw as co-hosts of the 2030 World Cup if Morocco is awarded the right to stage the final, the European nation’s main opposition party leader has said.

The centenary finals are set to be co-hosted by Morocco, Spain and Portugal, with the opening three matches due to be staged in South America as a nod to Uruguay’s hosting of the inaugural tournament in 1930.

FIFA has not confirmed which of the three main co-hosts will be awarded the final, only saying that a decision will be taken in due course.

However, Alberto Nunez Feijoo, the leader of the opposition People’s Party, insists Spain should back out of hosting the tournament if it is not chosen to stage the final.

“If Morocco ends up hosting the final, Spain should leave the organisation (of the tournament),” he told El Chiringuito TV.

Returning star makes 'up my game' admission amid striker battle

SPFL star targets goal rush after finally breaking his Premiership duck

He added on X in a post quoting the El Chiringuito interview: “Restoring our country’s pride also means not letting anyone walk all over us.

“The World Cup final belongs to Spain. The world’s top footballing nation is not humiliated. I will defend my country’s interests. In this too.”

The revamped Bernabeu Stadium or Camp Nou would be worthy contenders to host, but Morocco are continuing to push for the Grande Stade Hassan II in Casablanca, with a projected capacity of 115,000, to stage the showpiece game.

Morocco is currently staging the Under-17 boy’s World Cup, held an emergency meeting of the FIFA management board in August when FIFA president Gianni Infantino looked under threat and will also be the venue for next year’s FIFA Congress, where Infantino is hoping to secure a fourth term as president.

The hosts currently sit second from bottom in the Premier League table and are 15 points below the visitors, who are second behind Arsenal.

Here, Sports Mole details everything you need to know about how to follow Saturday's match.

The match will kick off at 5.30pm on Saturday afternoon in the UK.

The match is being played at Nottingham Forest's City Ground, which has a capacity of 31,212.

However, Forest have only picked up one point at home this season which is the worst record in the Premier League, while City have picked up three victories and one defeat on the road so far.

The Premier League match between Nottingham Forest and Burnley will be broadcast live on Sky Sports Main Event and Sky Sports Premier League, with coverage beginning at 5pm in the UK.

UK viewers can also stream the match through NOW with an All Sky Sports, Football or Day Membership.

Match of the Day will air highlights from the game on BBC One and BBC iPlayer on Saturday evening. The full highlights package will also be available on the BBC Sport website.

Sky Sports will post highlights on their app after full time and they will then be uploaded to the Sky Sports Premier League YouTube channel.

Forest are 19th in the Premier League on just six points from nine matches, and desperately need to pick up a positive result.

Head coach Oliver Glasner has come under increasing pressure in recent weeks, with Forest picking up one point from their last five league games.

It is also worth noting that Forest have only beaten City once in their last nine meetings across all competitions.

City, meanwhile, sit second on 21 points and are only three behind leaders Arsenal.

However, they head to Nottingham after suffering consecutive league defeats to Aston Villa and Brighton & Hove Albion.

City did bounce back from the Brighton loss on Wednesday, picking up a hard-fought 3-2 win over RB Leipzig in the Champions League.

#2210. Data estimates how much Cardiff City star Jack Moylan is worth ahead of potential January interest

Cardiff City have made a respectable start to life back in the Championship, although Brian Barry-Murphy’s side have struggled to find consistency in front of goal. Their problems in the final third have arguably left them lower down the table than their overall performances deserve.

However, those struggles have not affected summer recruit Jack Moylan. The Bluebirds decision to bring the attacking midfielder in from Lincoln City is already looking like an excellent piece of business.

The 25-year-old has quickly established himself as one of the Bluebirds’ most influential players, registering nine goals and three assists in his opening 14 Championship appearances. Moylan has also started every league fixture, highlighting just how important he has become to Barry-Murphy’s plans.

With Cardiff struggling towards the lower end of the table, his contributions have been particularly valuable. If he continues producing at this level, the Bluebirds may have to contend with growing interest in their summer signing when the January transfer window opens.

While the fee was reported as undisclosed upon Moylan’s announcement, reports have suggested that Cardiff City was an initial £3m which could rise to £5m.

The Bluebirds have tied Moylan down to a four-year deal running until the summer of 2028. With that in mind, if interest does arrive in January, they are in a strong position to ask for a hefty fee and profit.

However, the data suggests that Cardiff City may have overpaid to bring Moylan in this summer, which does not bode well for a potential sale. FootballTransfers’ estimated transfer value has the attacking-midfielder’s value at €2.2m, which is around £1.8m.

Off the current Cardiff City squad, Moylan has the second highest value with only Yousef Salech higher (€3.8m). Moylan’s value is also considerably lower than the only player who has more Championship goals then him Will Lankshear, who is valued at €20.7m.

A more direct comparison may be Blackburn Rovers striker Andri Gudjohnsen, who also has nine goals this campaign. His value is only €2.7m and much similar to Moylan’s, while being just a year younger.

Moylan’s statistics underline just how influential he has become for Cardiff City. His attacking numbers are particularly impressive, with 51 shots, 19 on target and 18 chances created.

He has also registered five big chances created, while his nine goals have come from an expected goals figure of 5.48, highlighting his clinical finishing and ability to outperform the underlying numbers.

The Irishman has delivered in some of Cardiff’s biggest performances, scoring twice in their 5-1 victory over Watford while impressing in a central striking role. He also scored a first-half hat-trick in the 3-3 draw with West Ham during the week.

Having progressed from Irish football to becoming a key figure in Lincoln’s League One title-winning campaign, Moylan has adapted impressively to the Championship.

#2209. England squad confirmed: Winners and losers from Thomas Tuchel's selection including Jobe Bellingham, Josh King, Cole Palmer, Kobbie Mainoo

By Ben Sully | 06 Nov 2026 13:23, Last updated: 07 Nov 2026 10:03

England boss Thomas Tuchel has confirmed his squad for the upcoming UEFA Nations League games against Croatia and Spain.

The Three Lions will head into their final two league phase matches in a strong position after taking nine points from their first four games.

Sitting in second spot in Group A3, England are all but guaranteed a quarter-final spot and could still finish above current leaders Spain.

England will return to action against Croatia at Wembley on November 12, before they head to Madrid for a meeting with the world champions on November 16.

Here, Sports Mole assesses the winners and losers of Tuchel's squad ahead of those Nations League fixtures.

Undoubtedly, the names of Jobe Bellingham and Josh King caught the eye when the squad was announced on Friday morning.

Jobe, younger brother of Jude Bellingham, has been a regular fixture at Under-21 level, having made 12 appearances for Lee Carsley's side.

But not many would have predicted his call-up to the senior squad, despite appearing to have taken an extra step in his performance levels with Borussia Dortmund this term.

The 21-year-old has started eight of Dortmund's matches across the Bundesliga and Champions League this term, including a strong display in the Der Klassiker last weekend.

Like Bellingham, King is another player who has earned his first senior call-up after enjoying plenty of football in Carsley's Under-21 side.

The 19-year-old has been one of the first names on Alvaro Arbeloa's team sheet this season, netting two goals and contributing one assist in his nine Premier League appearances.

King primarily plays as a number 10, but Tuchel has revealed he is keen "to see him on both wings" as he weighs up how best to utilise the youngster in his squad.

Ben White, meanwhile, has returned to the England squad for the first time since the March international window, when he was booed by some fans in games against Uruguay and Japan.

Some England supporters were seemingly reluctant to look past White's self-imposed exile from international football after an alleged falling out with Gareth Southgate's assistant Steve Holland at the 2022 World Cup.

After being omitted from the World Cup squad and missing the last international window through injury, White will be hoping to enjoy something of a fresh start when he links up with his England teammates next week.

Trent Alexander-Arnold is also a winner of Tuchel's selection despite struggling for game time at Real Madrid amid competition from Federico Valverde and Denzel Dumfries.

The former Liverpool man ultimately earned Tuchel's backing with the couple of encouraging performances he produced during the last international window.

Chelsea star Cole Palmer leads the list of notable omissions from Tuchel's 25-man selection for the games against Croatia and Spain.

Palmer, who missed out on selection for the World Cup, withdrew from the last England squad due to a small fitness issue.

At the time, Tuchel expressed his frustration with Palmer's withdrawal, claiming the attacker "misses too many camps".

Tuchel is not short of options for number 10s, and with concerns over Palmer's commitment to the national team, he has decided to pick other players who can provide cover for Jude Bellingham and compete for spots out wide.

The inclusion of Morgan Rogers, Morgan Gibbs-White, Eberechi Eze and King has also cost Phil Foden a place in the 25-man squad.

Foden has netted four goals and four assists in 11 competitive appearances for Manchester City this term, but he has struggled to deliver at international level and has not been selected since March.

In midfield, Manchester United's Kobbie Mainoo is the main notable omission, with Tuchel opting to hand Jobe his first England call-up instead.

Mainoo was left on the bench for England's World Cup campaign and then withdrew from the last squad with an unspecified injury.

With Tuchel placing importance on attending camps, the reasoning behind Mainoo's exclusion may be similar to Palmer's.

Nico O'Reilly, meanwhile, played the duration of September's defeat to Spain before he withdrew from the squad due to injury.

But he will not be involved this time around after being snubbed in favour of Lewis Hall and Djed Spence.

#2208. Bayern, Hoeness preoccupato per Musiala: "Non sappiamo se tornerà mai quello di prima"

Cresce la preoccupazione in casa Bayern Monaco per le condizioni di Jamal Musiala. Il talento tedesco, alle prese con problemi di salute che ne hanno condizionato la disponibilità, ha ripreso ad allenarsi con il gruppo e potrebbe tornare a disposizione dopo la sosta per le nazionali. A sollevare interrogativi sul suo recupero completo è stato però Uli Hoeness, presidente onorario del club bavarese, intervenuto ai microfoni della televisione pubblica BR. Le sue dichiarazioni lasciano emergere tutta l'incertezza sul futuro del centrocampista offensivo, che nel febbraio 2025 ha rinnovato il contratto fino al 2030, con un ingaggio di circa 25 milioni di euro a stagione.

Il dirigente ha parlato apertamente della situazione del nazionale tedesco, che secondo quanto riportato soffre di un disturbo neurologico funzionale con crisi di assenza: "Si trova in una situazione complicata: ha una malattia non facilmente curabile, ma che può essere tenuta sotto controllo in una certa misura con i farmaci. Tuttavia, non sappiamo se tornerà mai a giocare come prima". Hoeness ha poi affrontato anche l'aspetto contrattuale: "Continua a percepire lo stipendio, ma immaginate se il Bayern mettesse in discussione il suo contratto: le sue capacità attuali non corrispondono più a quanto previsto dal contratto stesso". Parole che evidenziano i timori del dirigente, senza che vi siano certezze sull'effettiva evoluzione delle condizioni del calciatore.

Nel corso dell'intervista, Hoeness si è soffermato anche sulla situazione di Vincent Kompany, lontano dalla panchina da oltre due settimane a causa di un problema di salute legato alla meningite. "Siamo molto preoccupati, anche se non credo sia così grave. Penso si sia trattato di una brutta influenza che si è trasformata in meningite. È curabile". Il presidente onorario ha quindi sottolineato il carico di lavoro dell'allenatore belga: "È sottoposto a un forte stress. Lavora dodici ore al giorno, sei giorni alla settimana". Infine, una previsione sul rientro: "Dopo la pausa per le nazionali, tra due o tre settimane, tornerà in panchina".

#2207. Genoa vs AC Milan - prediction, team news, lineups

By Jonathan O'Shea | 06 Nov 2026 13:24, Last updated: 08 Nov 2026 08:39

After a week of highs and lows, AC Milan will head towards the international break with a tough trip to Genoa on Sunday afternoon.

Before they clash at Stadio Ferraris, the hosts have actually claimed more points from their last five Serie A games than the inconsistent Rossoneri.

From derby delight to Europa League debacle, Milan have enjoyed and endured an eventful few days.

Soon after Ruben Amorim's unbeaten domestic record was ended by Bologna, his side entered the Derby della Madonnina as underdogs against reigning champions Inter Milan.

Yet, the Rossoneri surged into a two-goal lead through Adrien Rabiot and Christian Pulisic, before Diego Moreira's free-kick sealed an impressive win - their sixth in eight games against Inter.

Having closed within four points of their city rivals, Milan sit fifth in Serie A and could be contenders for the title - but that good feeling did not last for long.

Beaten for a third time in four Europa League fixtures, they went down 2-0 to Ferencvaros on Thursday night and may be facing an early exit.

It was also a rare failure to find the net, as Milan have scored 21 goals in their first 10 league games under Amorim - the best record of any new coach since club legend Cesare Maldini back in 1972.

The Rossoneri also remain undefeated on their travels around Italy - recording two wins and three draws so far - and they usually get the right result in Genoa.

Including a 2-1 victory at the very end of last term, Milan have won four straight away games against Sunday's hosts.

In fact, Genoa have won none of the last 10 league meetings, either home or away - a drought dating back to March 2020.

The Rossoblu may fancy putting a halt to that long winless run, having just ended their eight-year wait for three consecutive Serie A victories.

Much has changed since Daniele De Rossi swerved the sack during the last international break, as four wins from five have propelled his side up the standings.

Potentially facing a change of management and another relegation fight, they instead conjured up a stunning run of results.

Now occupying 11th place, Genoa continued their rise with a 2-1 win at Lecce last week, when ex-Milan man Junior Messias set up the opener before netting the second himself.

It has been 17 years since the Grifone posted four straight wins in a single top-flight campaign, but such a streak is now on the cards.

Three days on from a dismal defeat to Ferencvaros, it is likely that Amorim will make multiple changes.

For sure, Milan captain Mike Maignan will replace Lorenzo Torriani in goal, after serving a Europa League suspension on Thursday.

After his derby heroics, Moreira was initially benched in midweek, but he could also return to the starting XI; the Belgian winger has racked up seven goal involvements from his first six Serie A appearances.

Strahinja Pavlovic has been ruled out by a fitness issue, while Fikayo Tomori, Samuel Chukwueze, Mattia Gabbia, Alexis Saelemaekers and Ruben Loftus-Cheek are still unavailable.

By contrast, Genoa boss De Rossi may have a full squad to choose from, as Elias Havel and Lorenzo Venturino recently returned from long layoffs.

Milutin Osmajic is the hosts' top scorer with four league goals, while ex-Milan forward Stephan El Shaarawy seems to be returning to form.

The former Italy international - who made 83 appearances and scored 21 times for the Rossoneri in Serie A - has provided an assist in both of his last two games.

Bijlow; Marcandalli, Ostigard, Vasquez; Ehizibue, Frendrup, Traore, Ellertsson; Messias, El Shaarawy; Osmajic

Maignan; Gila, De Winter, Diawara, Estupinan; Musah, Modric; Moreira, Rabiot, Pulisic; Ramos

Allied to two cup wins at Stadio Ferraris, Genoa have won half of their first six home games in Serie A, so Milan will be put through a stern test.

While Amorim's men are leaking too many goals, De Rossi's side have found their shooting boots, and that could produce an end-to-end draw.

#2206. Top Gun, tears, thrills and the future script: Cardinale the leading actor in rollercoaster visit

When he touched down in Italy before the Udinese game, not even Gerry Cardinale himself will have foreseen the whirlwind week that followed.

The visit of the Milan owner was known about ahead of time, as were his motivations. Cardinale not only wanted to demonstrate his closeness to the team in a very important moment, but he also had a lot of off-field issues to tackle too.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

He would end up staying in the country for one week, something that has not been common. This is a different version of the RedBird Capital founder that we are seeing though, one that seems more hands-on than ever when it comes to steering the ship.

Or rather, in this case, it was a bit of a rollercoaster that he was at the front of. It started with a climb, continued with a steep drop, and then came the ascent which ultimately led into take-off on his flight back to America.

Things started quite well for Cardinale in his latest visit, as Milan went away to Udinese and won 2-0 thanks to goals from Christian Pulisic and Omari Hutchinson, bouncing back well from the defeat in Bournemouth a few nights prior.

Attention then shifted to the midweek meeting with a resurgent Bologna side at San Siro, and things did not go as well. Alphadjo Cisse’s brace was ultimately outshone by Samuel Mbangula’s hat-trick, as the Rossoblu scored three unanswered second-half goals to win 4-2.

It was a crash back down to earth, and a further reminder that this team remains very much a work in progress, with obvious limitations and shortcomings that can be exploited in quite a ruthless way. With the loss, Milan moved seven points off the leaders Inter and Lazio.

A few days ago, after the win in Udine, Cardinale had praised both the team and Ruben Amorim in a post-match interview. After the Bologna game, the owner not only didn’t speak, but left the stadium looking rather glum to say the least.

Sometimes, though, the curses can be a blessing too. Perhaps this was a necessary reminder for everyone – from Cardinale down to the players – that there is no shortcut with this project, and that there will be growing pains.

It cannot be argued though that it set a rather gloomy backdrop to everything that would come in the next few days, which makes what actually happened all the more remarkable.

The big day finally arrived on Thursday, just 24 hours after the Bologna match: Milan and Inter unveiled the renderings of their new stadium, along with design studios Foster+Partners and Manica.

The timing of the event – which took place at the Sforzesco in the city centre – felt deliberate. Just two days before the derby, Milan and Inter got together to release the first images of how the new stadium will look.

Some details had been known for some time about the specifics of the project, but visualising it is another thing. Moreover, they dropped the bombshell before hand that a day one naming rights partner had been found: Generali, thus making it the Generali Stadium.

❗ OFFICIAL: How #ACMilan and Inter's new home the 'Generali Stadium' will look.

— SempreMilan (@SempreMilanCom) October 29, 2026

Leaving aside any subjective thoughts on the design, it was a very proud moment for Cardinale. The American had made no secret of his desire to construct a new home for the two clubs, and he shed some light on things in an interview with CBS.

“You know, when we bought the club four years ago, I started working on this right away. And part of what we’re trying to do here is bring the best of America to Italian soccer, and specifically to AC Milan.

“It’s extraordinary: most people didn’t think we could accomplish all this, or even get to this point. Not only did we announce the stadium this week, but we also announced the largest naming rights deal in history. I think it’s a wonderful thing for Italian soccer.

“The message to everyone is that we’re coming back. Italian soccer truly deserves to be at the top again, and we’ll do everything we can through AC Milan to make that happen.”

Something about the build-up to the derby, right up until kick-off, felt different. It seemed as though there was a conscious decision to try and create a connection with the past, something that Cardinale himself appeared to lead.

For example, Sky reported that Cardinale invited about 60 people to a dinner the night before the game. It wasn’t just an opportunity to catch up, but also for the American to explain what he has planned for Milan, seeking the support of some club legends.

The likes of Adriano Galliani, Ricardo Kaka and the Serie A president Simonelli attended the dinner. Other notable Milan legends present were Marcel Desailly, Serginho, Cristian Brocchi, Mathieu Flamini, Mauro Tassotti, Filippo Galli and Daniele Massaro.

At the Milan Store, racks of shirts adorned printing with names like Gattuso, Nesta, Seedorf, Inzaghi, Pirlo, Ambrosini, Kaka and even Maldini. The tag line behind the campaign was to ‘celebrate the heroes of Athens’, but again it felt like constructed timing.

Going back to that CBS interview, the bold proclamations that Cardinale made on the broadcast certainly turned some heads.

“I remember George Steinbrenner telling me about twenty years ago, ‘Don’t get too emotionally involved in all this, don’t go into the locker room, don’t do those things’. But in this case, I got emotionally involved,” he said.

“For me, it became a personal issue. And I don’t know what it is: maybe it’s just that… look around, listen. This means so much to the people of Milan, it has a huge impact on them, and I feel a huge responsibility.

“AC Milan were at the top of the world, and we’re going to bring them back there. This will probably be one of the greatest things I’ll do in my career.”

Speaking of that link with the past, Kaka was presented with a trophy before kick-off to mark his induction into the Rossoneri Hall of Fame. It was a moment filled with emotion for all the fans present who chanted his name, while the Brazilian couldn’t hold back the tears as a montage video played on the screen.

Those words might just have fallen very flat had Milan not delivered on the field, but they did. No doubt fired up by the emotionally-charged atmosphere inside the stadium, Gerry Cardinale got to watch on as his players delivered a show.

Diego Moreira – the man he reportedly pushed to sign personally for €51m – was the start of the show. He got two assists from Christian Pulisic and Adrien Rabiot, two of his other favourites, before bending in a free-kick of his own. It was enough to blow the champions away.

Just 72 hours earlier, Cardinale had trudged through the mixed zone barely able to make eye contact with reporters after seeing his team implode against (at the time) bottom-half Bologna. On Halloween night, he had the smile of a child who had just scooped all the candy.

Speaking to QSVS, he got emotional: “What did you think of the boys? Every three days playing a game, going through the injuries, we get our full team back, and look at those guys. Those guys are winners. It brings tears to my eyes, it’s great right? The best night, and we’re going to build on this.”

🥲 “Look at those guys… it brings tears to my eyes.”

— SempreMilan (@SempreMilanCom) November 1, 2026

All of the pre-match hype, those ballsy interviews speaking of a desire to return to greatness, bringing legends back to invoke the nostalgia… it was a very risky move in hindsight. It could have backfired with a defeat, but the team delivered and gave everyone connected to the club immense pride.

So, within the space of four days and 180 minutes of football, Cardinale had witnessed both the floor and the ceiling of this team. The next mission – one he appears to be well aware of – is to bring the baseline up towards the obvious high potential Amorim’s group has.

Speaking to La Gazzetta dello Sport before the Inter game, the 59-year-old admitted that the squad is far from finished in his eyes, and that some further work is needed to try and get it up to the level required. He also invoked Top Gun to explain his bond with Amorim.

“We need to continue improving the squad because in the summer I wasn’t able to do everything I wanted. We’re rebuilding the team, and while I like the new arrivals, the ones who stayed, and the names I got rid of, it will probably take a couple more transfer windows to complete everything.

“We’re only a quarter of the way there, and the priority is to build a team that can deal with injuries. If you’re competing in European competitions and playing… Amorim Ball, which is high-tempo football, with quick transitions, where everyone runs between 9 and 11km per game, you have to rotate them.

“That’s why we’ll continue to be aggressive and opportunistic in the January transfer market, and I’ll continue to strengthen our bench. My style is to move decisively and quickly.

“What matters to me is getting what we want, players… that are Milan-worthy. I won’t waste money, but when I set my mind on signing a player, I do everything I can to succeed.”

The first big step will be to welcome a very important jigsaw piece to complete the management puzzle: Markus Krösche. The past few days also seemed to serve to iron out the remaining details to reach an agreement with the German, who will become the new Head of Football.

You may wish to remain sceptical, and that is absolutely warranted, but for the first time in a while there does seem to be the semblance of a path out of the rut. A more active owner, a talented squad with room to improve, a coach being backed, a key director incoming and a new stadium on the way.

Oh, and of course the bragging rights, which are heading back across the Atlantic Ocean with Cardinale while he no doubt draws up the itinerary for his next visit.

#2205. 19-year-old Southampton starlet showcasing exciting potential in European loan after early transfer interest

Southampton sent eight players out on loan this campaign. With a mixture of academy talents and players who are on their way out of St Mary’s, the Saints are seeing mixed success from the departed members of the squad.

Moses Sesay, Nicholas Oyekunle and Jay Robinson were the three teenagers to leave for the season. It’s the latter who Tonda Eckert will be monitoring closely after a breakout term last year in the Championship.

Robinson made 28 appearances for Southampton, scoring twice and providing an assist. Minutes were always going to be hard to come by for the 19-year-old this season. With a move to Monza secured, the attacker has plenty to prove in the Serie A.

Securing a loan move to Serie A is a positive sign of how much potential Robinson has to unlock. It’s safe to say that despite a slow start to life at Monza, the 19-year-old is becoming a key player for the Italian outfit.

The Southampton academy graduate had to wait until September to make his first start, and he impressed in a 1-0 win against Torino. Featuring in an attacking midfield role, he completed the most dribbles in the game with four and had nine touches inside the opposition’s box.

His first goal came in the next match, in which he completed 78% of his passes and was dispossessed only once. Former Saints boss Ivan Juric has shown full faith in Robinson, starting the teenager in three of the last four outings.

The 19-year-old’s best display came against Cagliari, where he scored once in a 4-0 win. Two out of his three dribbles were successful in the game, and he made three line-breaking passes. It has been an impressive loan stint for the youngster with Inter Milan registering their interest.

Southampton have the option to recall Robinson from his loan spell in January. However, given his promising spell so far, the Saints would be reckless to do so.

At left-wing, the South Coast outfit currently have the options of Leo Scienza and Sam Edozie, as well as Lewis Dobbin, who can play there too. Edozie is likely to depart in January, though, after putting in a transfer request late in the summer window.

This would ultimately leave Eckert’s side short of attackers in that position. However, they must avoid recalling Robinson to improve their depth. Even if Edozie were to leave, his exit would give Dobbin a better chance of breaking into the first team after just 409 minutes played.

Most importantly, it would do no favours to the 19-year-old’s development. The Southampton prospect has only recently become settled at Monza, and with his minutes increasing over the last couple of months, the South Coast outfit can’t afford to ruin his crucial playing time.

#2204. Cole Palmer pays England price as Thomas Tuchel explains ruthless omission

Thomas Tuchel says Cole Palmer is paying the price for his controversial withdrawal from England’s last camp.

The Chelsea forward was a notable omission from Tuchel’s squad for next week’s Nations League games against Croatia and Spain.

He was originally named in Tuchel’s group for September and October’s matches but withdrew due to a minor injury, despite playing 90 minutes in Chelsea’s final match, much to the annoyance of the German, who said he “misses too many opportunities to impress”.

The 24-year-old then featured in all four of his club’s Premier League matches ahead of this camp, but was overlooked by Tuchel, who handed maiden call-ups to Jobe Bellingham and Josh King.

“I guess everyone is desperate to play for England, and Cole is no exception,” Tuchel said.

“We’re speaking now about the player who is not in camp, and it counts when you are in camp.

“And I said last time, he misses too many opportunities to impress. It was a long camp. It was two and a half weeks, and the players and the boys who were in camp built something extraordinary and that’s why the credit goes to them.

“The trust is for them. If you are in camp, this is your chance to impress.

“So it’s not personal, and of course we will never assume that Cole is not in the mindset to come and desperate to play for his country. But the competition is on, and the players who were in camp did an amazing job and kept their place.

“The door is always open, but nothing has changed, and we decided to do this camp without him.”

Spain told to walk away from 2030 World Cup if Morocco gets final

Returning star makes 'up my game' admission amid striker battle

Aberdeen suffer major double blow as Stephen Robinson confirms injury lay-offs

Palmer was one of five withdrawals ahead of the last camp, with Declan Rice, Kobbie Mainoo, Tino Livramento and Marcus Rashford also pulling out.

Rice is the only one of that quintet recalled as Tuchel reiterated that he will always give preference to those who “walk the talk”.

“The best reactions to the withdrawals in the last camp from the players who were in camp was just to put the quality in and make it a successful camp,” Tuchel said.

“That’s the best foundation for the next camp, to have less withdrawals, which is actually happening now.

“So it’s always about the ones who are in camp.

“It’s always the ones who don’t withdraw and who walk the talk and come to camp and show that it matters and put the quality in. And this is always what we will reward.

“That was always the message, very, very early, since we took charge, that it matters if you are in camp and it matters what you do in camp and the last camp was an excellent one, so the trust and the belief is in the players that were there.”

Mainoo was also overlooked, despite returning to Manchester United’s side.

“I think Kobbie had a difficult spell now with his team and his club, and missed a lot, a lot of matches,” Tuchel said.

“And in the meantime, it was Jobe who impressed in the same level of time, and that’s exactly what we want.

“The door is always open for younger ones to overtake or to make their mark.”

#2203. Nico O'Reilly update: England omission explained and why it benefits all parties including Man City

By Oliver Thomas | 06 Nov 2026 13:27

Nico O'Reilly has been left out of the latest England squad due to fitness-related issues, Three Lions boss Thomas Tuchel and Manchester City manager Enzo Maresca have confirmed.

The 22-year-old was selected as England’s first-choice left-back for the 2026 World Cup and helped the Three Lions progress to the semi-finals in North America.

O’Reilly has earned 13 senior caps for England and has featured regularly under Tuchel, but a disrupted period at club level with Man City has led to his omission for November’s international break.

A midfielder by trade who has morphed into a left-back, O’Reilly has played the full 90 minutes just twice for Man City this season and has started seven games out of 15 in all competitions.

O’Reilly has predominantly been dealing with a persistent back problem that he sustained at the beginning of September, while a knee issue has also been causing him some discomfort.

Following England’s squad announcement on Friday morning, Tuchel discussed his full-back options ahead of UEFA Nations League fixtures against Croatia and Spain, and explained why O’Reilly will not be involved.

Inter Milan’s Djed Spence has returned to the senior fold at the expense of O’Reilly, while Lewis Hall and Jarrad Branthwaite are two other left-footed defensive options called up by Tuchel.

Meanwhile, Trent Alexander-Arnold, Jarell Quansah and Ben White are all set to battle for starts at right-back, with the latter returning to the Three Lions squad after an eight-month absence.

"Djed [Spence] is on the left side, we left Nico out because he's not fully, fully fit and needs to take his time to get fully fit and take care of his knee,” Tuchel told reporters.

"[Alexander-Arnold] was very good in the last camp, very good in the matches he played. Competition is on and he competes with Jarell and Ben for the position of right full-back."

Speaking at a press conference on Friday ahead of Man City’s Premier League trip to Nottingham Forest on Saturday, Maresca also shared his thoughts on O’Reilly’s fitness and his England omission.

"With Nico it's quite easy, some more problems, unfortunately. He's not 100%,” said the Citizens boss.

"In the last game (against RB Leipzig on Wednesday). I really liked him; I think he was playing a very good game, and this is what we need from Nico. Keep him fit because we know how good he is, and no doubt that in the future he will again be with the international team."

Asked whether O'Reilly needed rest over the international break, Maresca replied: "It’s what he needs, to use that break to do the right things in terms of therapy and this kind of things and be 100% over the international break”

O’Reilly’s absence from the England squad may well prove to be a blessing in disguise, as a much-needed break offers the youngster a crucial window to focus entirely on his recovery and return to full fitness.

Rather than risking a further setback on the international stage, the hiatus allows the starlet to build up his sharpness in a controlled environment, ensuring he is fully prepared for the gruelling club football schedule awaiting Man City.

With Maresca’s side facing a notoriously busy winter fixture list – 11 games before the end of the year – having a rejuvenated O'Reilly fresh and ready for domestic duties will provide a massive boost to the Citizens' squad depth.

Furthermore, his omission serves a dual purpose internationally, granting Tuchel the perfect opportunity to assess alternative left-back options and experiment with England's defensive depth in the closing stages of their Nations League group-stage campaign.

Hall is the most likely candidate to start at least one of England’s matches against Croatia and Spain this month, while Spence and Branthwaite provide alternative left-sided cover.

It remains to be seen whether O'Reilly will be involved against Nottingham Forest this weekend, or if he will instead return to action on November 21 when Man City take on Fulham in their first Premier League fixture after the international break.

#2201. Real Madrid head coach Jose Mourinho addresses Vinicius Junior's struggles at Bernabeu

By Matt Law | 06 Nov 2026 13:41, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 13:44

Real Madrid head coach Jose Mourinho remains confident that Vinicius Junior will rediscover his top form for the Spanish giants in the not too distant future.

Vinicius will complete a four-game domestic suspension on Saturday, when Real Madrid continue their La Liga campaign against Valencia.

The 26-year-old has had a tough campaign to date, only managing one goal and three assists in 12 appearances for Los Blancos in all competitions.

Vinicius again struggled to make his mark against AEK Athens in the Champions League, with the statistics showing how difficult a game it was for the Brazilian.

The attacker will once again be available in La Liga after the November international break, and Mourinho has said that he will continue to help Vinicius rediscover his best form.

“For me, the most important thing is the team. Never by my own will would I harm the team to benefit a player. When we return to normal with Vini Jr. being available, I’ll manage it," Mourinho told reporters during Friday's press conference.

"I want the team to think game by game, but as a coach, I have to think about the next matches and the sequence of games we have. The match against AEK was for Vini Jr., to give some rest to a player like [Arda] Guler, who had some issues.

“The most straightforward answer to your question is that a coach must do everything to have his players at the highest level, try different strategies, in-game or personal management, and always believe in the player’s true potential.

"A bad player will always be bad no matter how much you try to improve them. A good one has their bad moments but leaves the door open to return to their normal state.

"The game that Vini Jr. played in Athens - if it wasn’t Vini Jr. but a regular player - we’d all say he had a good game. Not incredible because his team didn’t play incredibly.

"We always expect the Vini Jr. who eliminated me from the Champions League, and that version exists, which we all expect. We must continue working with all strategies at all levels.

"Something important is the friendship of his teammates, without exception. When deciding who plays, the team is above the individual.”

The South American has scored 129 goals and registered 103 assists in 387 matches for Real Madrid during a highly-successful period.

Vinicius had been heavily linked with a move to Arsenal during the summer transfer window before ultimately signing a new long-term contract at Bernabeu.

#2200. GdS: Milan to sign record-breaking €500m deal with Adidas – the details

There has been talk about AC Milan moving to Adidas in recent weeks and that has now gone a step further, with details of the deal filtering.

By all accounts, next year will see Milan end their partnership with Puma. The two have worked together since 2018 – when the Rossoneri stopped working with the iconic German brand – and produced several kits in that time.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

However, with a focus on improving commercial value, Gerry Cardinale has now looked to the kit and decided to head back to what some may call home.

According to Gazzetta dello Sport, the Diavolo will sign a blockbuster deal with the three-stripe brand, worth €500 million across 10 seasons. In other words, they’ll earn a record-breaking €50m per season, as opposed to the reported €30m.

A deal with potential for ‘exponential growth’, it’s a €36m increase on the current contract with Puma – worth €14m per season – and significantly changes the value of the shirt. Looking elsewhere, Juventus’ deal with Adidas is believed to be worth €408m across 10 seasons, whilst Inter’s eight-year deal was worth €30m per season.

Above all, though, it’s a romantic return and a symbol of hope to return to better times. Throughout the history of their partnership, Milan have often been at the top, and it’s one of the most recognisable relationships in Italian (and European) football. Now, it’s coming back.

#2199. Arsenal: Mikel Arteta issues reassuring Ezri Konsa injury update before Hull City

By Ben Knapton | 06 Nov 2026 13:54, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 14:03

Arsenal manager Mikel Arteta has provided a reassuring injury update on Ezri Konsa ahead of Saturday's Premier League clash against Hull City.

The England international was taken off with 62 minutes gone against Slavia Prague in midweek, and Arteta revealed after the game that he was dealing with "some issues".

However, Konsa was named in the England squad for the upcoming November international break, suggesting that the unspecified problem he was dealing with against Slavia Prague was not serious.

Konsa was also spotted in team training earlier on Friday, and speaking to reporters in his pre-game press conference, Arteta alleviated fears over an injury to the centre-back.

"He is fine," Arteta said. "He was feeling much better today. We took him out as a precaution [against Slavia Prague] but yesterday and today he is feeling good."

Nevertheless, Arsenal can afford to play it safe with Konsa thanks to the presence of Cristhian Mosquera and William Saliba, the latter of whom made a matchday squad for the first time this season in Prague.

The Frenchman was an unused substitute following four months out with a back injury, though, and Arteta also revealed that the Gunners have been in close contact with France ahead of their international squad announcement later on Friday.

While Saliba is back in the ranks, Arteta has conceded that Bukayo Saka will need "weeks" to recover from quadriceps damage, suggesting that a comeback before December is unlikely.

"No, we talk about weeks... let's see after international break," Arteta added. "He's going to start hitting the pitch and start, actually have more activities, how he feels. But, unfortunately, he's going to be out for a while.

"I don't want to push these guys into too much pressure. They are doing their best to get him in shape. Bukayo is desperate to get back with the team, but it's still quite early."

Even though Konsa has been declared fit by Arteta, the Englishman's recent blow may have left the door ajar for Saliba to come straight back into the Arsenal ranks.

The 25-year-old is evidently capable of playing some minutes for the Gunners, hence his involvement in the matchday squad in midweek, but just how many minutes is another question entirely.

Given Saliba did not come off the bench in the Czech Republic, Arteta may see fit to introduce him as a substitute, which would also allow him to enjoy a proper reception on his comeback appearance.

Therefore, if Arteta does decide to err on the side of caution with Konsa, Mosquera should be first in line to join Gabriel Magalhaes in defence.

#2198. Neil McCann: Why nothing has changed in my approach to build strong Kilmarnock team

Neil McCann will continue his drive to see Kilmarnock “flourish” in the face of growing fan scrutiny.

The Ayrshire club are one point ahead of William Hill Premiership bottom side Hibernian, with nine points from 36 and having conceded 28 goals from 12 games amid concern and disgruntlement among a section of the Killie support.

McCann is preparing his players to take on a Celtic team who are four points ahead of Rangers at the top of the table, having played a game more and on the back of a morale-boosting 3-2 Europa League win over Besiktas on Thursday night

However, the Kilmarnock boss remains positive, and said: “Supporters will always give their opinion when they come into the stadium and they’ll voice it. They’re entitled to voice their opinion.

“My pressure, to be honest with you, is preparing our players tactically, physically, mentally.

Cole Palmer pays England price as Thomas Tuchel explains ruthless omission Spain told to walk away from 2030 World Cup if Morocco gets final

“And then when they take the field, I give them full autonomy to go and carry out all of those instructions, but play with the freedom and bravery that got us out of trouble last year.

“Nothing’s changed in my build-up. Nothing’s changed in my drive. Nothing’s changed in my approach to getting Kilmarnock to be a team that wants to put on a product on the pitch.

“A lot’s been made of ‘you can just win games and it’s all about winning games’. And I absolutely get that when it comes to management.

“Of course, it’s about results, very much, it’s a results-driven business. But I was given this job to get us out of bother last year.

“And we came in, the first thing wasn’t about, yes, results were important, but it was making sure that the team were capable and prepared to go and get those results.

“I think we are prepared to go and get those results. We just haven’t given our support enough good days to celebrate wins.

“But the work doesn’t stop. And it doesn’t deter me one bit from the work that I do on the training field.

“I’ll hopefully see a team flourish, get the results that I think their performances deserve. And then we can all come together with the unity that we showed last season and hopefully Kilmarnock will start climbing the table.”

#2197. Sheffield Wednesday team news and predicted XI as Dave Challinor takes charge vs Oldham Athletic

Sheffield Wednesday travel to the North West for their first game under Dave Challinor‘s management. An FA Cup first round clash against Oldham Athletic awaits SWFC, and the new boss will be eager to make a winning start.

Challinor is tasked with turning around some poor form of late as the Owls have gone four without a win, including consecutive draws under Matt Hamshaw’s temporary lead. Wednesday have a good history in this prestigious competition though, with 2021/22 the last time they got knocked out in the first round.

Oldham Athletic meanwhile sit 18th in League Two, so will be hoping for some cup joy. In their last game, Micky Mellon’s Latics won 1-0 against Northampton Town thanks to a second-half goal from Ollie Norburn.

Challinor has a handful of injury issues to deal with as he prepares to make his Sheffield Wednesday bow. Before the game, the new boss confirmed the Owls will be without Liam Cooper, Joe Lumley and Ali Al-Hamadi as their recent absences continue.

Cooper is a precautionary call, while Lumley and Al-Hamadi are expected to step up their returns next week.

Elsewhere, Billy Mitchell and Reece James are set to miss out, as are long-term absentees Louie Barry and Tyler Onynago. Sil Swinkels is suspended after his sending off against Blackpool, but Jamal Lowe will be available for selection having served his ban.

In better news, Di’Shon Bernard has been deemed fit to return for SWFC. Challinor is wary that his minutes still need to be managed but the former Manchester United youngster’s availability serves as a welcome boost.

Centre-back Ricardo Santos returned off the bench against Blackpool and is set to be in contention again.

Maynard-Brewer; Bernard, Santos, Weaver; Valery, Heskey, Bannan, Lowe; Slattery, Keillor-Dunn; Burstow

Challinor has mainly played with a 3-4-2-1 in his managerial career to date, so that is what formation is anticipated in his first Sheffield Wednesday game. However, he has used varied three-back setups as well as a 4-2-3-1 in the past.

Lumley’s absence means Ashley Maynard-Brewer continues in goal. He will be guarded by Ernie Weaver, and the fit-again Bernard and Santos in the back three with Yan Valery and Max Lowe selected as right and left wing-backs.

The fairly limited midfield depth means SWFC will likely persist with Barry Bannan and Jaden Heskey in the middle. Callum Slattery continues in a more advanced role alongside Davis Keillor-Dunn, who has now scored four goals in 10 games for the club.

Mason Burstow continues at striker despite Lowe’s return. The experienced striker will be hoping to impact the game off the bench though, alongside the likes of Sean Fusire, Jordi Liongola, Kieran Morrison and Harry Gray.

Oldham Athletic vs Sheffield Wednesday kicks off at 17:15 on Sunday evening and will be broadcast on TNT Sports 1.

#2194. Hearts vs Falkirk - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 06 Nov 2026 13:56, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 14:02

Meeting for the second time in less than two months, Falkirk travel to the Scottish capital looking to extend their winning run to five matches when they take on an inconsistent Hearts side.

The Jambos were in European action during the week but suffered their second defeat in five days, losing 3-1 to Thun in Switzerland, while the Bairns secured a memorable victory over Celtic to claim their first win against the Glasgow giants in 19 years.

After narrowly missing out on their first Scottish Premiership title since 1960, Hearts entered the summer facing a significant rebuilding task, having come closer than any club outside Celtic and Rangers to winning the championship since 1985.

Their preparations were further complicated when manager Derek McInnes departed to take charge the Gers, bringing several key players with him.

Wouter Vrancken was appointed as the new Jambos head coach in June, becoming the first Belgian to take charge of the club, but he has so far been unable to maintain the momentum built under McInnes.

After 23 matches, the former Sint-Truidense manager has recorded just eight victories, alongside six draws and nine defeats, leaving Hearts third in the Scottish Premiership, out of the Scottish League Cup and 32nd in the Conference League league-phase table.

Hearts travelled to Thun on the back of Sunday's Scottish League Cup semi-final defeat to city rivals Hibernian, meaning Vrancken's men were in need of a positive result to lift the mood around the club.

However, a poor defensive display saw the Jambos suffer a 3-1 defeat against their Swiss opponents, leaving them without a victory in the Conference League after three matches, having also drawn with Nordsjaelland and lost to Trabzonspor in their previous European outings.

As for visiting Falkirk, they make the short journey east in considerably better spirits after securing a memorable victory over league leaders Celtic last weekend, courtesy of first-half goals from Ethan Ross and Scott Tanser.

The victory at the Falkirk Stadium was their first against the Bhoys in almost two decades and continued an impressive run of form following their difficult start to the 2026-27 campaign.

John McGlynn's side have now won four consecutive Scottish Premiership matches, a run that began in mid-October following a defeat to Dundee, and have climbed the table from second-bottom to fourth.

Their impressive turnaround has also been built on improved defensive performances, with only two sides in the division conceding fewer than their 12 goals, while only Rangers have collected more points across the last six Premiership matchweeks.

Bairns' superb run has also earned manager McGlynn the Premiership Manager of the Month award for October, and his side will now look to overcome a Hearts team they have already beaten this season, having secured a 2-1 victory in September.

Despite their inconsistent performances and recurring defensive issues, Hearts have also been hampered by a significant injury list during the opening months of the season.

An injury-hit squad travelled to Switzerland in midweek and returned with further concerns after Stephen Kingsley was forced off shortly after the hour mark against Thun, ruling him out of Sunday's encounter alongside 10 other players.

Harry Milne, Calvin Miller and Blair Spittal are among those currently sidelined, with Milne not expected to return to action until August 2027.

As for the visitors, Falkirk also head into the weekend with a large number of players unavailable through injury.

Despite the joy following their victory over Celtic, Ryan Strain was forced off in the early stages of that match and will play no part this weekend.

He joins Leon McCann, Aodhan Doherty, Joseph Gardner, Ethan Laidlaw, Keelan Adams and Aidan Nesbitt in the treatment room.

Reus; Gillies, Fagan-Walcott, Findlay, McCart; Kerjota, Altena, Mendy, McPake; Guendouz, C. Braga

Bain; Allan, Neilson, Henderson, Tanser; Melniks, Spencer; Ross, Barbrook, Earnes; Murrell

Despite arriving in Edinburgh in contrasting form, the two sides are relatively evenly matched in the league table, and we expect Falkirk to provide a stern test for their hosts.

Hearts will be keen to respond after successive defeats, but with McGlynn's men enjoying an excellent run of results, we anticipate a closely contested encounter with the points ultimately being shared.

#2193. Blackburn Rovers defender has had an excellent breakout year – but patience is required: Opinion

Blackburn Rovers have had a largely frustrating 2026, but there has been reason to believe. The return of Tony Mowbray has seen the club build on Michael O’Neill’s work and pushed the club up into mid-table.

Another huge positive over the last year has been the emergence of Tom Atcheson. The 20-year-old becoming one of Rovers’ most encouraging academy success stories, with his development over the past 12 months earning him recognition on the international stage.

His form has also attracted attention from several top clubs. Atcheson was the subject of enquiries from Manchester City, Liverpool, Chelsea, Newcastle United, Sunderland and Crystal Palace in the summer window.

The 20-year-old has progressed from a promising youngster to a regular Championship starter and an increasingly important member of Northern Ireland’s defence. His senior breakthrough has not simply been a reward for potential, either.

Consistent performances at club level have given O’Neill plenty of reasons to trust him, with Atcheson starting all four of Northern Ireland’s Nations League fixtures in September and October, helping them record a run of clean sheets.

That is a significant achievement for a player who only made his senior international debut in March. Having joined Blackburn at under-12 level, Atcheson enjoyed a loan spell with Marine before making his Championship debut against Millwall in December 2025.

14 league appearances followed last season, and he has since established himself as a regular under Tony Mowbray.

Atcheson’s underlying numbers this campaign suggest there is plenty to like, although they also highlight why expectations should remain measured. His average FotMob rating of 6.54 also indicates that, despite his impressive international progress, he has not consistently been among the Championship’s standout defenders.

There have been occasional reminders of his inexperience, too. His first international start against Guinea in June ended with a goal and a red card, while a difficult 45 minutes against Swansea last month preceded a minor groin problem that forced him to miss the subsequent Stoke fixture.

Nevertheless, Blackburn have every reason to be encouraged. Atcheson has already demonstrated that he can translate Championship reliability into international performances, and his physical profile makes him an appealing long-term prospect.

The Premier League comparisons may be premature, but his trajectory is undeniably positive. Another six to 12 months of consistent performances could take his reputation, and his market value, to another level.

#2192. Ex-Celtic star tells Martin O'Neill why he's on receiving end of 'flak with love'

Joe Hart has told Martin O'Neill why he is getting "flak with love" amid Celtic's stuttering form.

Scrutiny at the club is growing and growing after a draw with Dundee United was followed up by defeat to Falkirk. Previously, the domestic form had been almost flawless, but for losing to Rangers.

Fans are now concerned their lead at the top of the table is precarious and are questioning the direction of the club even more.

Former Parkhead goalkeeper Hart says that scrutiny is always going to be there and it does not affect O'Neill's standing with the support.

He told The Overlap: "It’s flak with love, and that doesn’t make any sense. Of course, we know it doesn’t make any sense, but Martin O’Neill will be, and is, absolutely adored. But he knows what he signed up for as a Celtic manager, and he would expect to be getting a lot of questions asked about him.

Neil McCann: Why nothing has changed in my approach to build strong Kilmarnock team Cole Palmer pays England price as Thomas Tuchel explains ruthless omission

And that’s not saying he’s not up to the task or he’s not the man for the job, but that’s what comes with the territory. If you really understand Celtic, you understand that if you’re not borderline perfect domestically and challenging in Europe, you’re going to get it.

"You’re going to get it because they care and they’re intelligent football fans. And, there’s a massive disharmony with the board. The fans really don’t understand what the direction of the club is. And obviously the board have got their opinions in terms of the success that they had and continue to have.

So, it is difficult. That was really shining brightly mid-season last season, but then Martin came back in and the team started to win and it goes away. It’s amazing what winning can do, and at the moment they’re not.

"They had a difficult result at Falkirk. I think they drew at home to Dundee United. And then this Besiktas game felt ominous. It felt like there was going to be a riot, or not even a riot. Do you know what the worst feeling you can have in a stadium is? And I very rarely felt it up there. It wasn’t anger, it was people going, ‘Pfft, we lost again.’ That is the worst feeling you could have.

"You’d rather have someone that impassioned, that embroiled as a Celtic fan. Like I say, domestically, I lost very, very few games, but no matter what run you’d been on, you could feel it. It wasn’t the majority, but you’d feel some people’s whole life and whole livelihood is proudly Celtic.

"And if you don’t perform in that shirt, you’re going to get it. I’ve still got friends up there, and they were flat going into the game. If Celtic had lost, they wouldn’t have been angry, they’d have been like, ‘Oh, God.’ And that’s not a nice feeling. I talked about it for Michael [Carrick], but I think it’s a little bit different up there, because they’re still top of the league.

"If they can take the atmosphere that they generated towards the end of the game last night into the league game this weekend, I think it’s Kilmarnock, then it could be a massive spark to go again.”
