#2189. Man United injury news: Michael Carrick delivers Patrick Dorgu, Carlos Baleba updates ahead of Aston Villa showdown

By Matt Law | 06 Nov 2026 13:58, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 14:01

Manchester United could have Patrick Dorgu back in their squad for Sunday's Premier League game against Aston Villa, but Carlos Baleba is facing "a number of weeks" out.

Dorgu returned to training earlier this week, with the left-back recovering from the hamstring issue that he suffered while representing Denmark during the last international break.

The 22-year-old was not involved against Roma in the Champions League but does have a chance of making the squad against Villa on Sunday.

The news on Baleba is not as positive, though, with the Cameroon international facing a lengthy period out with the hamstring issue that forced him off the field against Roma.

"Carlos, we don't know exactly, but it'll be a number of weeks. It's definitely not this week, and it won't be anytime over the international break. It'll be a little bit longer than that," Carrick told reporters during Friday's press conference.

"Patrick has done a little bit of training this week. You've seen him train with us at the start of the week, very, very small. So we've got to be patient with Pátrick.

"He's had a good period of time now with a couple of hamstrings that we need to manage that and be patient with that over the next few weeks as well. But he's slowly getting back with us, so we'll have to see whether he can be involved at the weekend or not."

Dorgu has not played for Man United since the clash with Fulham on September 20, providing one assist in his five appearances for the club this season.

Manuel Ugarte, Benjamin Sesko, Luke Shaw and Marcus Rashford are again set to miss out this weekend through injury, while Lisandro Martinez is suspended.

Man United will be bidding to build on their 3-2 success over Roma in the Champions League when they welcome Villa to Old Trafford on Sunday.

"It's up to us to take it forward; it was a fantastic night as a one-off night. It was a good night for us all, but nothing has been achieved yet, and it's one moment we need to use to move forward," Carrick said on Friday afternoon.

"When you watch the game (Roma) back, the first half wasn't so bad. There were moments when we left ourselves short, and we need to fix them. The first half against Chelsea was very good. We're showing signs of improvement, no doubt, and we need to take that forward.

"It's a positive reaction, it's not like all of a sudden we're getting carried away. I know what the players are capable of. We just haven't proved it enough lately. The boys have trained really well, I'm not just saying that for the sake of saying it."

Man United are currently 17th in the Premier League table, picking up only seven points from their opening nine matches of the campaign.

#2188. FIFA presidency: Election date, candidates and Gianni Infantino’s re-election chances

By Axel Clody | 06 Nov 2026 14:05

The FIFA presidential election takes place in March next year and the activity behind the scenes is already busy.

Gianni Infantino, the current president, is seeking a fourth and final term. Under pressure from the controversy over his attempt to sell the commercial rights to the World Cup, he is expected to face competition from two names.

Gianni Infantino has a second challenger for the FIFA presidency ? Japan FA honorary president Kozo Tashima joins former Tottenham defender Ramon Vega in declaring his intention to run, with the vote set for 18 March 2027 in Rabat, Morocco. https://t.co/rTC9Y0T88S

The most recent to announce his intention to stand for football’s top job was Japan’s Kozo Tashima, honorary president of the Japan Football Association (JFA), which he led between 2016 and 2024.

The official, who has also been a member of FIFA’s technical council since 2015 and played football in the 1980s, stressed that he has the support of the JFA and that his experience at the head of the national federation will help him take on the challenge.

Before Tashima, former defender Ramon Vega, who played for Tottenham, Watford and Celtic, announced last month his own intention to stand for the FIFA presidency, with a platform of strengthening governance. Since retiring in 2004, he has worked in finance and asset management. In 2019, Vega tried to stand but failed to secure the minimum support from federations.

To be a candidate for the leadership of football’s governing body, the backing of five federations is required, something that Vega and Tashima still need to seek. The deadline for candidacies to be made official is 18 November.

In the middle of this year, behind the scenes, Infantino was negotiating the sale of 20% of the commercial rights to FIFA’s competitions, including the women’s and men’s World Cups, to private investors.

Among the possible interested parties was Jared Kushner, son-in-law of Donald Trump, the president of the United States, who has a strong relationship with the FIFA president, the man responsible for giving him a peace prize last year.

The plan, however, was revealed by the The Times, which prompted an immediate negative reaction from almost all of the continental confederations. UEFA (Europe), CONCACAF (North and Central America) and the AFC (Asia), as well as several FIFA executives and advisers, condemned the idea and forced Infantino to backtrack.

The European body in particular was the loudest critic of the Italian-Swiss executive, pushing for his resignation or removal and threatening a boycott of FIFA competitions, an idea that was later dropped.

With time, however, the pressure eased and the president, with the ‘machine on his side’ for re-election, made his move. Last month, the Asian Football Confederation renewed its alliance with Infantino and now supports his re-election, with Japan, through Kozo Tashima, the exception.

He already has the backing of the CAF (Africa) and CONMEBOL (South America), and nothing changed with a recent new scandal, in which the French newspaper Le Monde revealed that FIFA paid $4m (around £3m) in personal taxes for its president in 2024, 2025 and 2026.

The current president is the favourite to have his mandate extended until 2031. Gianni Infantino has been in office since 2016 and was re-elected in 2019 and 2023. In theory, he could not seek a fourth term, but because he initially took over after the resignation of Sepp Blatter, that was not considered an election.

There are 211 national associations affiliated to FIFA, with 55 of them from UEFA, 54 from the CAF, 46 from the AFC, 35 from CONCACAF, 11 from the OFC (Oceania) and 10 from CONMEBOL. Each has the right to one vote, which is secret.

If there is a candidate against Infantino, whoever receives more than 50% of the votes is elected. If there are three or more in the contest (as could happen with Tashima and Vega), two thirds of the votes are needed in the first round. If that figure is not reached, the candidate with the fewest votes is eliminated until only two contenders remain, with the winner decided by simple majority.

The 2027 election will be held on 18 March at the 77th FIFA Congress in Rabat, the capital of Morocco.

#2187. Kiernan Dewsbury-Hall: Thomas Tuchel makes feelings clear on Everton star after England omission

By Oliver Thomas | 06 Nov 2026 14:10, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 14:11

Thomas Tuchel has admitted that Everton midfielder Kiernan Dewsbury-Hall was “very close” to earning a maiden England call-up for November’s international break.

The 28-year-old has established himself as an important first-team player for the Toffees since joining in August 2025, recording 10 goals and eight assists in 44 appearances.

Dewsbury-Hall is one of the first names on the Everton teamsheet under manager David Moyes, starting all nine Premier League matches in an advanced midfield position this season and chipping in with two goals.

The former Leicester City and Chelsea man delivered two standout performances for the Toffees in back-to-back wins over Newcastle United in the EFL Cup and Premier League, enhancing his case for recognition at senior international level.

Moyes is a huge admirer of Dewsbury-Hall and recently stated that the midfielder is “knocking on the door” for an England call-up.

"Thank goodness I don't have to pick the England team because they have so many good players to choose from,” Moyes told reporters on Thursday.

"Kiernan's form is getting back to his levels of last year. I hope he does [get the call-up]. He'll certainly be knocking on the door."

Tuchel has since confirmed that Dewsbury-Hall is among a group of 55 players who are in contention for England selection, but he has narrowly missed out on this occasion.

Speaking about Dewsbury-Hall at press conference on Friday, Tuchel said: "Very good player, very good season. A very good football player overall.

"In the way we play now, I don't see him in the central midfield in the build-up, but in the finishing part as an offensive No.8 or almost as a No.10.

"He's in our 55 and a standout player for Everton. We see his quality and he's a nice watch because he's a very good football player. He was very close.”

Tuchel has opted to call up Borussia Dortmund’s Jobe Bellingham and Fulham’s Joshua King for the first time, while Everton’s James Garner, Jude Bellingham, Elliot Anderson, Declan Rice and Myles Lewis-Skelly are five more midfielders selected.

Dewsbury-Hall is not the only notable midfield omission from Tuchel’s latest squad, with Cole Palmer, Kobbie Mainoo and Phil Foden also left out, while Alex Scott is absent due to injury.

From a selfish perspective, many Everton fans would prefer to see Dewsbury-Hall rest up and not sustain an injury during international duty, but many others believe that the midfielder now deserves his chance to represent the Three Lions.

Dewsbury-Hall is expected to start for Everton in tonight’s Premier League clash at home against Frank Lampard’s Coventry City.

Everton duo Garner and Jarrad Branthwaite are also expected to feature against Coventry before linking up with the England squad, with Tuchel previously highlighting the qualities of both players.

"I am very happy, [Garner is] our mini [Federico] Valverde. He's getting there and he played very, very good,” Tuchel said in March.

"I was quite sure that he would play well because he has this natural confidence and he's very physical; he won a lot of balls.

“There are some situations where he can be a bit more open and drive through midfield with the ball with a little bit more confidence… But I am very impressed with the way he trained, and the way he behaves.”

On Branthwaite, Tuchel said in September: “We’ve had Jarrad on our radar for a long time. We see big potential in him... He’s fast, tall and has a strong left foot. It’s good to see him over a long period and give him a chance.”

England will face Croatia and Spain in their final two UEFA Nations League fixtures, playing host to the former at Wembley on November 12 and travelling to Madrid to take on the latter three days later.

#2186. What Alex Neil must learn from Millwall’s 3-0 defeat to West Brom heading into the international break

Millwall have had a terrible opening third of the 2026/27 season. The Lions suffered their seventh loss of the campaign and their second this week as West Brom were 3-0 victors at The Hawthorns on Saturday.

Alex Neil’s side did well to nullify a WBA side who were unbeaten in five before the weekend inside the first 45 minutes. However, the second half saw the South London outfit capitulate defensively as they made it 27 goals conceded this term.

Millwall will welcome the pause in Championship action for two weeks as they reset. There are certainly many lessons learned from their latest defeat that the Lions must use as fuel over the international break to come back stronger heading into the busy winter period.

This time last year, when Millwall headed into the international break, they were sat 6th in the table and just four points off the top two. Now after their second consecutive 3-0 loss, the Lions are 16th in the Championship and six points from the top eight.

Neil’s side have scored six more goals than they did this time last season. However, they have conceded seven more times. Saturday highlighted the gulf in quality and momentum between a side in real play-off contention like West Brom and a team in disarray like Millwall.

The Baggies had an xG of 2.98 whereas the South London outfit only had an xG of 0.62. The Lions also had just nine touches inside the opposition’s box and won just 48% of their ground duels.

All three goals Millwall conceded against West Brom weren’t entirely at the fault of the New Zealand international. However, it’s safe to say that the Lions won’t achieve anything this season with him as their number one.

Max Crocombe has conceded 22 goals this term out of 10 games and has a save percentage of 55.1%. He is currently averaging 2.2 goals conceded per match, and the 33-year-old is struggling to cope with the demands of the Championship.

Against WBA, the New Zealand international made an error leading to a goal and made just three saves. With Lukas Jensen reported to be nearing a return to fitness and his save percentage standing at 100% from his two games this term, Neil won’t select Crocombe again if he doesn’t have to.

One positive Millwall can take from the clash against West Brom is the return of duo Josh Coburn and Tairyk Arconte. Coburn played 54 minutes before being substituted, whilst Arconte featured for the last 10 minutes.

Both players have been crucial to the limited success Neil’s side has had this season. Coburn especially had a strong start to the term before an injury sidelined him for two months. He has won 69.2% of his aerial duels this campaign and has an xG on target of 3.14.

Arconte was also missed by the Lions. He has netted three times in nine games for Millwall in his debut Championship season. The 22-year-old has a successful dribble rate of 60% and has made 45 touches inside the opposition box.

#2185. GdS: ‘Make way for the youth’ – why Amorim will opt for rotation vs. Ferencvaros

After a difficult October, it seems Ruben Amorim will give some of his more prominent AC Milan players a break in the Europa League.

The game against Ferencvaros is the first game where Amorim will really get a chance to rotate. He couldn’t in the first game against Benfica, and a reaction was needed against RB Salzburg – though a few youngsters did play.

Against Bournemouth, it also wasn’t considered. However, it will now be an opportunity to rotate at San Siro, in a game where Milan will be expected to win.

After the derby – and just generally a long month – Amorim is expected to name several changes to his XI on Thursday night. As Gazzetta dello Sport (seen below) writes, it will be a ‘younger’ side on Thursday, but not to snub the Europa League. Indeed, the average age is expected to be at least four years younger than the 28-year-old squad that played against Inter.

Lorenzo Torriani will replace the suspended Mike Maignan, Luka Modric will be replaced by Ardon Jashari and then there will be other starts for Davide Bartesaghi, Alphadjo Cisse and even ‘the veteran’ Omari Hutchinson.

Sankhoun Diawara could start again, this time in place of Strahinja Pavlovic, and maybe Pervis Estupinan and Filippo Terracciano will start.

Additionally, Christian Comotto and Francesco Camarda could be seen, with one a possible starter. It seems more likely that it’d be the striker at this moment, though.

#2184. Cina, che crollo del Chengdu: perde 12 punti in 4 giornate e regala il titolo al Dalian Yingbo

Una delle rimonte più incredibili degli ultimi anni si è consumata nel campionato cinese, dove il Chengdu Rongcheng ha gettato alle ortiche un titolo che sembrava ormai conquistato. A quattro giornate dalla conclusione, la formazione allenata da John Aloisi vantava addirittura 12 punti di vantaggio sulla più immediata inseguitrice. Sarebbe bastato un solo punto nelle ultime quattro partite per festeggiare il trionfo, ma è arrivato un crollo tanto improvviso quanto inspiegabile: quattro sconfitte consecutive e primato sfumato proprio all'ultima giornata. Ad approfittarne è stato il Dalian Yingbo, protagonista di una rimonta che resterà nella storia del calcio cinese.

Il verdetto definitivo è arrivato nell'ultimo turno, quando il Chengdu è stato sconfitto per 3-2 dallo Shandong Taishan. Un risultato che ha aperto definitivamente le porte al sorpasso del Dalian, vittorioso per 3-1 contro il Tianjin. Per la formazione di Aloisi è stata la quarta sconfitta consecutiva, mentre i rivali hanno centrato il quinto successo di fila, completando una rimonta che fino a poche settimane prima sembrava impossibile. Le due squadre hanno così concluso il campionato appaiate a quota 52 punti.

A determinare l'assegnazione del titolo sono stati gli scontri diretti, entrambi vinti dal Dalian Yingbo, che ha potuto così festeggiare il primo campionato della propria storia. Un'impresa straordinaria, costruita attraverso un finale di stagione perfetto e favorita dal tracollo della capolista. Per il Chengdu Rongcheng resta invece una delusione difficilmente cancellabile: perdere un campionato con dodici punti di margine a quattro giornate dal termine, senza riuscire a conquistare neppure un pareggio, rappresenta un epilogo destinato a essere ricordato a lungo.

#2183. Man United transfer news: Premier League striker known to Michael Carrick emerges as 'shock' Red Devils target

By Matt Law | 06 Nov 2026 14:21, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 14:24

Manchester United are reportedly considering making a shock move for Fulham striker Rodrigo Muniz during the January transfer window.

Benjamin Sesko is expected to be absent until the start of 2027 with an ongoing shin problem, although he would face even longer on the sidelines if it is ultimately decided that an operation is required to fix the issue.

Joshua Zirkzee has made nine appearances for Man United this season but is not in the club's long-term plans, and a move away from the club next year is expected.

Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha have both played through the middle this season, but it is understood that the 20-time English champions want to bring in a new centre-forward during the January transfer window.

Leeds United's Dominic Calvert-Lewin and Al-Ahli's Ivan Toney have both recently been linked with moves to Old Trafford.

Meanwhile, it is understood that Jean-Philippe Mateta is also an option given that his contract with Crystal Palace will expire at the end of the campaign.

According to The Sun, Fulham's Muniz has now emerged as a potential option.

The report claims that Muniz's lack of football this season could see him leave in January, and Man United are considering him as a short-term fix.

The 25-year-old played under Michael Carrick at Middlesbrough during a loan spell in 2022-23, so he is known to the Man United head coach.

Muniz has scored twice in 10 appearances for Fulham this season, but he has failed to find the back of the net in seven outings in the Premier League.

The Brazilian has a record of 29 goals and four assists in 132 matches for Fulham in all competitions, including 18 goals and three assists in 85 Premier League games.

Muniz's best season at Fulham came in 2024-25, when he netted 11 times in 36 appearances in all competitions, but he only struck once in 25 matches for his London club last term.

It remains to be seen whether Carrick stays in charge of Man United heading into the January transfer window, but the Red Devils must add a forward to their squad in the winter.

#2182. Galli reveals details of Cardinale’s speech at legends dinner: “Heartfelt and sincere”

Before the Inter game, AC Milan invited several legends to a dinner hosted by Gerry Cardinale to honour them.

In recent years, there has maybe been an avoidance of celebrating the club’s history. Iconic players have been overlooked and the past hasn’t been at the forefront. Of course, the present takes precedence, but it’s almost like the rich history of Milan has been overlooked.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

There have been a few nods to the past, as well as a few kits with historic hints. Yet, you’d be lying if you said that the club were completely honouring the legends that had represented them before.

Now, things are seemingly changing. The Hall of Fame presentation was the most public display of affection, but Cardinale also hosted a dinner for the club’s legends. In attendance of the dinner was Filippo Galli, and he’s offered some information on what happened, praising the American for the event, as MilanNews relays from his interview with Gazzetta dello Sport.

“He spoke for a good half hour, and I found it to be heartfelt and sincere. I’d particularly emphasise the mea culpa for the mistakes made. I’d say it’s a good start; his words seemed heartfelt, and not necessary. But there’s still a long way to go.

“He insisted that he will continue to invest. I’d say he understands how soccer works in Italy, compared to the American one, where everything is more relaxed.

“He then spoke kindly of Berlusconi’s career at the club, emphasising, however, that Berlusconi would now be dealing with a changed world, where financial sustainability becomes vital. Now we’re waiting for something to happen…

“Right now, there’s a January transfer window to address, a technical area to sort out and define. There’s a need to establish high-level governance; otherwise the coach will be left alone, and if by chance the results don’t arrive…

“Did this dinner organised by Cardinale surprise me? Actually, yes, we weren’t expecting it. The invitation came from Milan a couple of weeks earlier. For us, these are opportunities to meet up with former teammates, to represent Milan. And this, regardless of who owns the club.”

#2181. Mika Baur identifies the Celtic challenge he is ready to rise to

Mika Baur is enjoying the demands of being a Celtic player – and was delighted to see the team display their fighting spirit and belief as they broke their Europa League duck.

The 22-year-old impressed as Celtic came from behind to beat Besiktas 3-2 on Thursday and get off the mark in the Europa League at the fourth time of asking.

The Hoops were heading for a seventh defeat in 11 matches when they fell behind early in the second half but the introduction of Callum Osmand, Camilo Duran and Colby Donovan on the hour mark sparked the team into life.

Manager Martin O’Neill recently admitted that some new players were still adapting to the demands of playing for Celtic but Baur and Duran have quickly proved they were successful summer signings.

Baur arrived from Paderborn after helping them to promotion to the Bundesliga and made his Germany debut last month, but moving to Scotland has been a new experience.

Neil McCann: Why nothing has changed in my approach to build strong Kilmarnock team Cole Palmer pays England price as Thomas Tuchel explains ruthless omission

“To play every three days is different for me,” the midfielder said. “I think I have played the same amount of games now that I had last season by the winter break.

“But I like it. That’s what you want. You want to play all the time, more playing than training. I am really enjoying it.

“And I think the pressure to win is a good kind of pressure. It’s better than (trying) not to lose. So I really enjoy it.”

Baur has shown pace and skill on the ball but his pressing in the final stages drew massive appreciation from the crowd on Thursday.

“First of all, I want to work hard for the team, for myself, that I can prove myself and I can play on the stage, help the team, put in a shift, hard work,” he said.

“That’s what I have to do first. And then after this, the skill comes, playing with the ball. But first of all, it’s showing heart on the field. I was really happy that it was appreciated.”

The win over the Turkish side felt like it could be a major step in Celtic’s season as they bounced back from losing the opening goal and from a late equaliser, with Duran netting the winner seconds later.

“The second half was pure heart, pure fight,” Baur said. “I’m not here for a long time, but I think that’s what it’s all about. This win feels unbelievable because we deserved it. We fought for it.

“The team is really, really close together. We know that we had a tough couple of weeks, but you can feel it in the training sessions, how the team is working together, the feeling, the believing is always there. And it was there on the pitch. That’s what we have to show week by week, every three days.

“It was not just our win. The atmosphere was great. I’m really, really thankful for every supporter in the stadium. They gave us a lot of energy. We all fight together. That makes me happy.”
